## What your diagnostic says

| Group | Mean spoof score | Share above 0.5 |
|---|---|---|
| LibriSpeech clean (human) | 0.16 | 18% |
| LibriSpeech + phone-like | 0.01 | 0% |
| LibriSpeech + noise 15 dB | 0.01 | 0% |
| LibriSpeech + room reverb | 0.58 | 60% |
| **Your voice, near mic** | **0.91** | **94%** |
| **Your voice, far** | **0.93** | **96%** |

That changes my diagnosis, so I'm correcting it. I said it might be a general failure on non-ASVspoof audio. It isn't: clean human speech from LibriSpeech mostly scores low, and noise and phone-band *lower* the score. The problem is **specific to your recordings**. There are four candidates, and the data can't tell them apart yet:

1. **Your laptop microphone path.** An ASUS laptop (Armoury Crate is in your process list) often applies "AI noise cancelling" or audio enhancements before any program sees the sound. Processed, denoised speech has artifacts that look synthetic.
2. **Room acoustics.** Reverb pushed LibriSpeech from 16% to 60% above 0.5, so a normal room hurts.
3. **Language or accent.** 24 of your 36 clips are Hinglish or Hindi, and the model only ever heard English.
4. **Your voice.** Less likely, but possible.

You can find out which with a few controlled checks (Step B below).

## The plan: "spoof stream v2" (2-3 days)

You said you have no fake-speech samples, so Step A is making them. I verified the tools: `edge-tts` (Microsoft's free neural voices, including `en-IN-NeerjaNeural`, `en-IN-PrabhatNeural`, `hi-IN-SwaraNeural`, `hi-IN-MadhurNeural`) and `gTTS`. They need internet while generating, and both are unofficial wrappers of online services, which is fine for a student project, but say so in the report.

### Step A: make fake speech from your own lines (30 minutes)

```bash
pip install edge-tts gTTS
```
Save as `audio/make_tts.py` and run `python -m audio.make_tts` from `rakshavoice/`. It reads your synthetic text batches in `nlp/data/synthetic/`, and produces about 160 clips with **edge-tts** (the training engine) and about 80 with **gTTS** (a *different* engine that you hold back as the "unseen generator" test). English and Hinglish lines use Indian-English voices, and Hindi lines use Hindi voices. I tested the text-selection logic offline; the generation itself needs your internet, so I haven't run it.

```python
import asyncio, glob, os, random, subprocess, time
import numpy as np, pandas as pd, soundfile as sf, librosa

OUT = "audio/data_v2/fake"
SR = 16000
random.seed(0)
VOICES = {"english": ["en-IN-NeerjaNeural", "en-IN-PrabhatNeural"],
          "hinglish": ["en-IN-NeerjaNeural", "en-IN-PrabhatNeural"],      # romanized Hindi read by an Indian-English voice
          "hindi": ["hi-IN-SwaraNeural", "hi-IN-MadhurNeural"]}
N_PER_VOICE = {"english": 25, "hinglish": 25, "hindi": 30}                  # sentences per voice (edge-tts)
N_GTTS = {"english": 25, "hinglish": 25, "hindi": 30}                       # sentences per language (gTTS, kept as the unseen engine)


def pick_texts():
    df = pd.concat([pd.read_csv(f) for f in glob.glob("nlp/data/synthetic/batch*_*.csv")], ignore_index=True)
    df = df.drop_duplicates("text")
    df = df[df.text.str.split().str.len().between(6, 22)]                   # sentences of a natural length
    return {lang: df[df.lang == lang].text.tolist() for lang in VOICES}


def to_wav16k(src, dst):
    try:
        y, sr = sf.read(src, dtype="float32", always_2d=True); y = y.mean(axis=1)
    except Exception:                                                        # soundfile build without MP3 support
        tmp = dst + ".tmp.wav"
        subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-y", "-loglevel", "error", "-i", src, "-ac", "1", "-ar", str(SR), tmp], check=True)
        y, sr = sf.read(tmp, dtype="float32"); os.remove(tmp)
    if sr != SR: y = librosa.resample(y, orig_sr=sr, target_sr=SR)
    sf.write(dst, y, SR)


async def make_edge(texts, manifest):
    import edge_tts
    os.makedirs(f"{OUT}/edge", exist_ok=True)
    for lang, voices in VOICES.items():
        pool = texts[lang][:]; random.shuffle(pool)
        for vi, voice in enumerate(voices):
            for k, text in enumerate(pool[vi * N_PER_VOICE[lang]:(vi + 1) * N_PER_VOICE[lang]]):
                name = f"{voice}_{lang}_{k:03d}"
                try:
                    await edge_tts.Communicate(text, voice).save(f"{OUT}/edge/{name}.mp3")
                    to_wav16k(f"{OUT}/edge/{name}.mp3", f"{OUT}/edge/{name}.wav"); os.remove(f"{OUT}/edge/{name}.mp3")
                    manifest.append({"path": f"{OUT}/edge/{name}.wav", "engine": "edge-tts", "voice": voice, "lang": lang, "text": text})
                except Exception as e:
                    print("skip", name, type(e).__name__, e)
                await asyncio.sleep(0.2)


def make_gtts(texts, manifest):
    from gtts import gTTS
    os.makedirs(f"{OUT}/gtts", exist_ok=True)
    for lang in VOICES:
        pool = texts[lang][::-1][:N_GTTS[lang]]                              # take from the other end so texts differ from edge-tts
        code, tld = ("hi", "co.in") if lang == "hindi" else ("en", "co.in")
        for k, text in enumerate(pool):
            name = f"gtts_{lang}_{k:03d}"
            try:
                gTTS(text=text, lang=code, tld=tld).save(f"{OUT}/gtts/{name}.mp3")
                to_wav16k(f"{OUT}/gtts/{name}.mp3", f"{OUT}/gtts/{name}.wav"); os.remove(f"{OUT}/gtts/{name}.mp3")
                manifest.append({"path": f"{OUT}/gtts/{name}.wav", "engine": "gtts", "voice": f"{code}-{tld}", "lang": lang, "text": text})
            except Exception as e:
                print("skip", name, type(e).__name__, e)
            time.sleep(0.5)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    texts = pick_texts()
    print({k: len(v) for k, v in texts.items()}, "texts available")
    manifest = []
    asyncio.run(make_edge(texts, manifest))
    make_gtts(texts, manifest)
    pd.DataFrame(manifest).to_csv("audio/data_v2/manifest_fake.csv", index=False)
    print(len(manifest), "fake clips written; manifest saved")
```
If it says it can't read the MP3s, install ffmpeg (`winget install Gyan.FFmpeg`) and reopen the terminal. **Listen to 5-6 of the clips** to check they sound sensible (Hindi lines in Devanagari should sound like Hindi, and the romanized Hinglish ones should sound passable).

### Step B: find the cause with three controlled checks (about 1 hour)

**B1. Split your clips by language** (free, 1 minute). I appended a small block to `audio/channel_check.py` that reports your scores separately for English, Hinglish and Hindi lines. Add this to the end of the file and rerun it:

```python
# 4) same person, by language of the line you read (en = English, hg = Hinglish, hi = Hindi)
for lang in ("en", "hg", "hi"):
    report(f"Your voice, lines in '{lang}'", own(f"nlp/test_clips/{lang}_*__clean.wav") + own(f"nlp/test_clips/{lang}_*__far.wav"))
```
If English is low and Hindi/Hinglish are high, the cause is language, not your microphone.

**B2. Turn off microphone enhancements** (10 minutes). In Windows Settings, go to System, Sound, Input, click your microphone, and set **Audio enhancements** to Off (if you see it). Also open **Armoury Crate or MyASUS** and turn off **AI noise cancelling**. Then re-record 6 clips with `python -m nlp.record_clips`, tagged `raw`, and score them. If they drop a lot, the enhancements were the cause. (If Armoury Crate lets you choose a different microphone mode, try that too.)

**B3. Replay test with real versus fake** (15 minutes, needs Step A). This is the most important check, because it copies your demo (speaker to microphone). Save as `audio/replay_test.py`, set the speaker volume to about 60%, keep the room quiet, and run `python -m audio.replay_test`. It plays 20 LibriSpeech clips (real) and 20 TTS clips (fake) through your speakers, records them with your microphone, scores them, and prints the AUC for direct and replayed audio.

```python
import glob, random
import numpy as np, sounddevice as sd, librosa
from sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score
from audio.spoof_stream import score_chunk

SR, N = 16000, 20
random.seed(0)

def load4(path):
    y = librosa.load(path, sr=SR, mono=True)[0]
    y = y[:4 * SR] if len(y) >= 4 * SR else np.pad(y, (0, 4 * SR - len(y)))
    return (y / (np.max(np.abs(y)) + 1e-9) * 0.5).astype(np.float32)           # same loudness for every clip

def replay(y):
    pad = np.zeros(SR // 2, dtype=np.float32)
    x = np.concatenate([pad, y, pad]).reshape(-1, 1)
    rec = sd.playrec(x, samplerate=SR, channels=1, dtype="float32"); sd.wait()
    r = rec[:, 0]; return r[SR // 2: SR // 2 + 4 * SR]

real = [load4(f) for f in random.sample(glob.glob("voice/data/LibriSpeech/test-clean/*/*/*.flac"), N)]
fake = [load4(f) for f in random.sample(sorted(glob.glob("audio/data_v2/fake/edge/*.wav")), N)]
print("replaying", len(real) + len(fake), "clips through the speaker (about", int((len(real) + len(fake)) * 5.5), "seconds) ...")

res = {}
for name, clips in [("real", real), ("fake", fake)]:
    res[name + "_direct"] = [score_chunk(y) for y in clips]
    res[name + "_replay"] = [score_chunk(replay(y)) for y in clips]
for k, s in res.items():
    s = np.array(s); print(f"{k:12s} n={len(s)}  mean={s.mean():.2f}  median={np.median(s):.2f}  share above 0.5 = {np.mean(s > 0.5) * 100:3.0f}%")
for cond in ("direct", "replay"):
    y = np.array([0] * N + [1] * N); s = np.array(res["real_" + cond] + res["fake_" + cond])
    print(f"AUC real-vs-fake, {cond:6s}: {roc_auc_score(y, s):.3f}   (0.5 = scores carry no information)")
```
I syntax-checked it but couldn't run it (no speaker or microphone here). The sound will play through your speakers for about 3.5 minutes.

**How to read B3:**

| Result | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Direct AUC high (above about 0.9) and replay AUC near 0.5 | The model works on clean files but the speaker-to-microphone path destroys the cue. Training must include replayed audio (the plan below does) |
| Direct AUC already low | The model doesn't recognise TTS from these engines. More fake data and retraining needed |
| Both high | Good news: the model works, and the only problem was your own recordings (B1 and B2) |

### Steps C-E (after B), outline

| Step | What | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **C. Build training set v2** | Real: LibriSpeech, plus 40-60 more lines **you read** (from the same text pool, so real and fake share content). Fake: edge-tts clips. Hold out: gTTS (an unseen generator), some LibriSpeech speakers, and some of your lines. Put **every** clip, real and fake, through the same MP3 compression, because MP3 is the codec the TTS tools output, and otherwise the model can cheat by detecting MP3 artifacts instead of fake speech. Add noise, reverb and phone-band versions of both classes, plus a replayed copy of a subset | Laptop |
| **D. Retrain** | Upload to Kaggle, extract wav2vec2 features, train the head on ASVspoof (cached) **plus** the new data, with early stopping on a new-domain dev set | Kaggle |
| **E. Evaluate and install** | Report EER per condition: ASVspoof eval (should stay about 2.6%), held-out gTTS (unseen generator), held-out human speakers, your own held-out lines, and the replayed set. Install the new `spoof_head.pt`, rerun `channel_check.py`, and update the page | Both |

**One stretch item:** cloning your own voice (with your consent) with an open model such as XTTS-v2 on a Kaggle GPU would give fake speech *in your voice*, which shows whether the spoof detector and the voiceprint check can tell a clone from you. It also matches your demo scenario. I'd treat it as optional after Step E, and I haven't verified the current install steps for that model.

I'll write Steps C-E scripts once Step B tells us what the data needs.

## What your three checks show

| Check | Result | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| **B1 language** | English 0.96, Hinglish 0.95, Hindi 0.86 | Not a language problem. Your English clips score highest |
| **B2 enhancements off** | Raw recordings 0.86 (before: 0.91 near, 0.93 far) | Small drop only. Microphone enhancements are not the main cause |
| **B3 direct** (clean files) | Real 0.18, fake 0.98, **AUC 0.97** | The model catches TTS it never saw, in clean files |
| **B3 replay** (speaker to microphone) | Real 0.12, fake 0.07, **AUC 0.325** | After the speaker-to-microphone path it cannot tell real from fake |

**Two cautions on B3.** The direct AUC of 0.97 may be partly a shortcut: the TTS clips came out of an MP3 pipeline and LibriSpeech is FLAC, so the model might be hearing the MP3 codec instead of synthetic speech. And with 20 clips per class, 0.325 is "no better than chance", not proof of an inverted detector.

**What it adds up to:** the spoof score depends mostly on the *recording channel*, not on whether the speech is real. Human speech into your microphone scores high (0.86-0.96). Anything replayed through a speaker scores low (0.07-0.12), real or fake. I don't know why a close-microphone human voice looks "fake" to the model, and neither language nor enhancements explains it. One published analysis of ASVspoof 2019 found that models trained on it can latch onto shortcuts such as silence length, and I suspect something like that here, but I haven't proven it for your model.

**Why this matters more than it first looks:** in your demo, both the genuine caller *and* the cloned voice reach the laptop through a phone speaker into the microphone. So the replay row is the one that decides whether the spoof stream is usable, and right now it carries no information.

## The fix: retrain on data from that exact channel (spoof stream v2)

The same data teaches the model that your direct-microphone voice is real, and that replayed real and fake speech differ. I'll set expectations honestly: it's possible that the speaker-to-microphone path destroys the cues fake-speech detectors rely on, so after retraining, replay accuracy might stay modest. If so, that is a legitimate finding for the report, and the fusion network will rely more on the intent and voice streams.

### Step C1: Build the "direct" training set (10 minutes)

Install ffmpeg first, so every clip goes through the same MP3 codec and the model can't use the codec as a shortcut: `winget install Gyan.FFmpeg`, then reopen the terminal. Save as `audio/prep_v2.py` and run `python -m audio.prep_v2` from `rakshavoice/`.

It trims every clip to 4 s of voiced speech and matches loudness, so silence length and volume can't be shortcuts. It then splits by **speaker** (LibriSpeech), by **line** (your own recordings), and by **engine** (edge-tts for training, gTTS held out entirely as the unseen generator). I tested the splitting logic on stand-in audio, not on your real files.

```python
import glob, os, random, re, shutil, subprocess
import numpy as np, pandas as pd, soundfile as sf, librosa

SR, SEC = 16000, 4
OUT = "audio/data_v2/direct"; os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
random.seed(0); rng = np.random.RandomState(0)
UTT_PER_SPEAKER = 6
USE_MP3 = shutil.which("ffmpeg") is not None            # push every direct clip through the same MP3 codec the TTS engines use

def voiced_crop(y, sec=SEC):
    y, _ = librosa.effects.trim(y, top_db=30)
    n = int(sec * SR)
    if len(y) < int(0.75 * n): return None               # too short after trimming
    if len(y) < n: y = np.pad(y, (0, n - len(y)))
    s = rng.randint(0, len(y) - n + 1)
    y = y[s:s + n]
    return (y / (np.max(np.abs(y)) + 1e-9) * 0.5).astype(np.float32)

def mp3_roundtrip(path):
    tmp = path + ".mp3"
    subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-y", "-loglevel", "error", "-i", path, "-ar", "24000", "-b:a", "48k", tmp], check=True)
    subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-y", "-loglevel", "error", "-i", tmp, "-ac", "1", "-ar", str(SR), path], check=True)
    os.remove(tmp)

def read16k(path):
    y, sr = sf.read(path, dtype="float32", always_2d=True); y = y.mean(axis=1)
    return y if sr == SR else librosa.resample(y, orig_sr=sr, target_sr=SR)

rows = []
def add(cid, y, cls, source, split, lang, who):
    p = f"{OUT}/{cid}.wav"; sf.write(p, y, SR)
    if USE_MP3: mp3_roundtrip(p)
    rows.append({"id": cid, "path": p, "cls": cls, "source": source, "split": split, "lang": lang, "who": who, "condition": "direct"})

# ---- real: LibriSpeech speakers (28 train / 4 dev / 8 test) ----
spk = sorted(os.listdir("voice/data/LibriSpeech/test-clean"))
for i, s in enumerate(spk):
    split = "train" if i < 28 else "dev" if i < 32 else "test"
    files = glob.glob(f"voice/data/LibriSpeech/test-clean/{s}/*/*.flac"); random.shuffle(files)
    k = 0
    for f in files:
        y = voiced_crop(read16k(f))
        if y is not None: add(f"libri_{s}_{k:02d}", y, "real", "librispeech", split, "english", s); k += 1
        if k >= UTT_PER_SPEAKER: break

# ---- real: your own recordings (split by line so the same sentence never appears in two splits) ----
lines = sorted({re.sub(r"__.*", "", os.path.basename(f)) for f in glob.glob("nlp/test_clips/*__*.wav")})
for li, line in enumerate(lines):
    split = "train" if li < 12 else "dev" if li < 14 else "test"
    for f in sorted(glob.glob(f"nlp/test_clips/{line}__*.wav")):
        y = voiced_crop(read16k(f), SEC)
        if y is not None:
            tag = os.path.basename(f).split("__")[1].replace(".wav", "")
            add(f"own_{line}_{tag}", y, "real", "own", split, {"en": "english", "hg": "hinglish", "hi": "hindi"}.get(line[:2], "?"), "me")

# ---- fake: edge-tts (70 % train / 15 % dev / 15 % test) and gTTS (all test = unseen engine) ----
for engine, folder in (("edge-tts", "edge"), ("gtts", "gtts")):
    files = sorted(glob.glob(f"audio/data_v2/fake/{folder}/*.wav")); random.shuffle(files)
    for j, f in enumerate(files):
        y = voiced_crop(read16k(f))
        if y is None: continue
        frac = j / max(1, len(files))
        split = "test_unseen_engine" if engine == "gtts" else "train" if frac < 0.7 else "dev" if frac < 0.85 else "test"
        lang = re.search(r"_(english|hinglish|hindi)_", os.path.basename(f))
        add(f"fake_{engine}_{os.path.basename(f)[:-4]}", y, "fake", engine, split, lang.group(1) if lang else "?", os.path.basename(f).split("_")[0])

df = pd.DataFrame(rows); df.to_csv("audio/data_v2/manifest_direct.csv", index=False)
print("MP3 codec equalisation:", "ON" if USE_MP3 else "OFF (ffmpeg not found; direct clips keep a codec difference between real and fake)")
print(df.groupby(["cls", "source", "split"]).size().to_string())
```
Expect roughly 240 LibriSpeech clips, up to about 160 of your own windows, and about 240 fake clips. If your own-clip count is far lower, tell me.

### Step C2: Replay everything through the speaker (about 1 hour in total, in chunks)

Save as `audio/replay_batch.py`. Set the speaker volume to about 60% and keep the room quiet. Each run replays 150 clips (about 14 minutes) in shuffled order, so every run covers all classes, and it skips clips already recorded:

```python
import argparse, os
import numpy as np, pandas as pd, sounddevice as sd, soundfile as sf

SR, PAD = 16000, 0.5
ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(); ap.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=150); ap.add_argument("--tag", default="laptop")
args = ap.parse_args()
OUT = f"audio/data_v2/replayed_{args.tag}"; os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
man = pd.read_csv("audio/data_v2/manifest_direct.csv")
done = {os.path.basename(f)[:-4] for f in os.listdir(OUT)}
todo = man[~man.id.isin(done)].sample(frac=1, random_state=0).head(args.limit)     # shuffled, so every run covers all classes
print(len(done), "already recorded;", len(todo), "to do now (about", int(len(todo) * 5.6 / 60), "minutes)")

for n, r in enumerate(todo.itertuples(), 1):
    y, _ = sf.read(r.path, dtype="float32")
    x = np.concatenate([np.zeros(int(PAD * SR)), y, np.zeros(int(PAD * SR))]).astype(np.float32).reshape(-1, 1)
    rec = sd.playrec(x, samplerate=SR, channels=1, dtype="float32"); sd.wait()
    seg = rec[int(PAD * SR): int(PAD * SR) + len(y), 0]
    rms = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(seg ** 2)))
    if rms < 0.003:
        print("  too quiet (rms %.4f): raise the speaker volume or move the microphone closer" % rms); continue
    sf.write(f"{OUT}/{r.id}.wav", seg, SR)
    if n % 25 == 0: print(" ", n, "/", len(todo))
print("done. Total replayed clips:", len(os.listdir(OUT)))
```
Run `python -m audio.replay_batch --limit 150` about four times, until it says the total is near the number of direct clips. Don't talk or type loudly during runs, since the microphone records everything. Listen to three of the files in `audio/data_v2/replayed_laptop/` to check they sound like speech, not silence or only noise. (I syntax-checked this script but it needs your speaker and microphone, so it is untested.)

For the demo path itself (a **phone** speaker), run the same script again later with a phone playing the clips, tag `phone`. That is a refinement, so don't block on it.


## Step 2: The notebook (GPU on, Internet on)

Create a new notebook, **add three datasets as inputs**: `rakshavoice-spoof-v2`, your features dataset (`rakshavoice-spoof-features`), and your model dataset (`rakshavoice-spoof-model`, so the old head can be compared). Paste the code below as five cells, split at the `# =====` lines.

I unit-tested the numpy-only parts (the helpers in Cell 3: the EER function, bootstrap intervals, the sampling weights and the test-subset selection) on stand-in data, and syntax-checked the whole file. The GPU, wav2vec2 and training parts have **not** been run, so expect to fix a small error on first run and send it to me.

```python
# ===== Cell 1: setup (GPU on, Internet on) =====
import os, glob, json, random
import numpy as np, pandas as pd, soundfile as sf, librosa, torch, torch.nn as nn
from transformers import Wav2Vec2Model, Wav2Vec2FeatureExtractor
from sklearn.metrics import roc_curve, roc_auc_score

dev = "cuda"
SR, LEN = 16000, 64000
random.seed(0); np.random.seed(0); torch.manual_seed(0)
rng = np.random.RandomState(0)

MAN = glob.glob("/kaggle/input/**/manifest_direct.csv", recursive=True)[0]
V2 = os.path.dirname(MAN)
OLD = os.path.dirname(glob.glob("/kaggle/input/**/train_X.npy", recursive=True)[0])
REPLAY_DIRS = sorted(os.path.basename(d) for d in glob.glob(f"{V2}/replayed_*") if os.path.isdir(d))
man = pd.read_csv(MAN)
print("clips in manifest:", len(man), "| replay folders:", {d: len(os.listdir(f"{V2}/{d}")) for d in REPLAY_DIRS})
print(man.groupby(["cls", "source", "split"]).size().to_string())
if not REPLAY_DIRS:
    print("WARNING: no replayed_* folder found. Training on direct audio only will NOT fix the speaker-to-microphone case.")

fe = Wav2Vec2FeatureExtractor.from_pretrained("facebook/wav2vec2-base")
w2v = Wav2Vec2Model.from_pretrained("facebook/wav2vec2-base").to(dev).eval()


# ===== Cell 2: wav2vec2 features for the new clips (same recipe as Phase 1) =====
def read(path):
    y, sr = sf.read(path, dtype="float32", always_2d=True); y = y.mean(axis=1)
    return y if sr == SR else librosa.resample(y, orig_sr=sr, target_sr=SR)

def fix(y):
    y = y[:LEN]
    return np.pad(y, (0, LEN - len(y))) if len(y) < LEN else y

def noisy(y, snr_db=15):
    return (y + rng.randn(len(y)) * np.std(y) / (10 ** (snr_db / 20))).astype(np.float32)

def phone(y):
    y = librosa.resample(librosa.resample(y, orig_sr=SR, target_sr=8000), orig_sr=8000, target_sr=SR)
    return noisy(y, 25)

def room(y, rt=0.3):
    n = int(rt * SR); ir = rng.randn(n) * np.exp(-6.9 * np.arange(n) / n); ir[0] = 1.0
    out = np.convolve(y, ir / np.sqrt(np.sum(ir ** 2)))[:len(y)]
    return (out / (np.max(np.abs(out)) + 1e-9) * np.max(np.abs(y))).astype(np.float32)

AUGS = {"noise": lambda y: noisy(y, 15), "phone": phone, "room": room}      # training clips only

def features(wavs, bs=16):
    out = []
    for i in range(0, len(wavs), bs):
        x = fe([fix(w) for w in wavs[i:i + bs]], sampling_rate=SR, return_tensors="pt").input_values.to(dev)
        with torch.no_grad():
            hs = w2v(x, output_hidden_states=True).hidden_states
        out.append(torch.stack([h.mean(1) for h in hs], 1).half().cpu().numpy())
    return np.concatenate(out)

meta, wavs = [], []
for r in man.to_dict("records"):
    y = read(f"{V2}/direct/{r['id']}.wav")
    meta.append({**r, "condition": "direct"}); wavs.append(y)
    if r["split"] == "train":
        for name, fn in AUGS.items():
            meta.append({**r, "condition": "aug_" + name}); wavs.append(fn(y))
    for d in REPLAY_DIRS:
        p = f"{V2}/{d}/{r['id']}.wav"
        if os.path.exists(p):
            meta.append({**r, "condition": d}); wavs.append(read(p))
meta = pd.DataFrame(meta)
X_new = features(wavs)
y_new = (meta.cls == "fake").astype(int).values
np.save("/kaggle/working/v2_X.npy", X_new); meta.to_csv("/kaggle/working/v2_meta.csv", index=False)
print(X_new.shape, meta.condition.value_counts().to_dict())
del wavs


# ===== Cell 3: helpers (numpy only) =====
def eer(y, s):
    fpr, tpr, _ = roc_curve(y, s); fnr = 1 - tpr
    i = np.nanargmin(np.abs(fnr - fpr)); return float((fpr[i] + fnr[i]) / 2)

def eer_threshold(y, s):
    fpr, tpr, thr = roc_curve(y, s); i = np.nanargmin(np.abs((1 - tpr) - fpr)); return float(thr[i])

def boot_ci(y, s, n=500):
    rs = np.random.RandomState(0); pos, neg = np.where(y == 1)[0], np.where(y == 0)[0]; e, a = [], []
    for _ in range(n):
        i = np.concatenate([rs.choice(pos, len(pos)), rs.choice(neg, len(neg))])
        e.append(eer(y[i], s[i])); a.append(roc_auc_score(y[i], s[i]))
    return [round(float(v) * 100, 1) for v in np.percentile(e, [2.5, 97.5])], [round(float(v), 3) for v in np.percentile(a, [2.5, 97.5])]

def make_weights(group, cls, cond, share):
    """Sampling probability per training item: each group gets its share, classes are balanced inside a group,
    replayed audio counts 3x and augmented audio 0.5x relative to direct audio."""
    cw = np.array([3.0 if c.startswith("replayed") else 0.5 if c.startswith("aug_") else 1.0 for c in cond])
    w = np.zeros(len(group))
    for g, sh in share.items():
        for c in (0, 1):
            m = (group == g) & (cls == c)
            if m.sum(): w[m] = sh * cw[m] / cw[m].sum() / 2
    return w / w.sum()

def masks(meta, cond):
    c = (meta.condition == cond).values
    lib_t = c & (meta.source == "librispeech").values & (meta.split == "test").values
    own_t = c & (meta.source == "own").values & (meta.split == "test").values
    edge_t = c & (meta.source == "edge-tts").values & (meta.split == "test").values
    gtts_t = c & (meta.source == "gtts").values & (meta.split == "test_unseen_engine").values
    return {"real: held-out LibriSpeech speakers": lib_t, "real: your held-out lines": own_t,
            "fake: edge-tts, unseen sentences": edge_t, "fake: gTTS (unseen engine)": gtts_t}


# ===== Cell 4: train the v2 head =====
class SpoofHead(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, L=13, D=768):
        super().__init__()
        self.w = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(L))
        self.net = nn.Sequential(nn.LayerNorm(D), nn.Linear(D, 256), nn.GELU(), nn.Dropout(0.3), nn.Linear(256, 1))
    def forward(self, x):
        a = torch.softmax(self.w, 0)[None, :, None]
        return self.net((x * a).sum(1)).squeeze(-1)

def load_old(split):
    p = f"{OLD}/{split}_X.npy"
    return (np.load(p), np.load(f"{OLD}/{split}_y.npy")) if os.path.exists(p) else (None, None)
Xtr_a, ytr_a = load_old("train"); Xdv_a, ydv_a = load_old("dev"); Xev_a, yev_a = load_old("eval"); Xit_a, yit_a = load_old("itw")

tr_new = (meta.split == "train").values
X_all = np.concatenate([Xtr_a, X_new[tr_new]]); y_all = np.concatenate([ytr_a, y_new[tr_new]]).astype(int)
group = np.array(["asv"] * len(Xtr_a) + ["new"] * int(tr_new.sum()))
cond = np.array(["asv"] * len(Xtr_a) + list(meta.condition[tr_new]))
NEW_SHARE = 0.5                                         # half of every batch comes from the new in-domain data
prob = make_weights(group, y_all, cond, {"asv": 1 - NEW_SHARE, "new": NEW_SHARE})
wt = torch.tensor(prob, dtype=torch.float32)
Xg = torch.tensor(X_all).to(dev); yg = torch.tensor(y_all, dtype=torch.float32).to(dev)
print("training items:", len(X_all), "| new in-domain:", int(tr_new.sum()), "| expected share of new data per batch:", NEW_SHARE)

@torch.no_grad()
def score(head, X, bs=512):
    head.eval(); out = []
    for i in range(0, len(X), bs):
        out.append(torch.sigmoid(head(torch.tensor(X[i:i + bs]).float().to(dev))).cpu().numpy())
    return np.concatenate(out)

def dev_score(head):
    parts = {"asv": eer(ydv_a, score(head, Xdv_a))}
    d = (meta.split == "dev").values
    for name, c in [("direct", (meta.condition == "direct").values), ("replay", meta.condition.str.startswith("replayed").values)]:
        m = d & c
        if m.sum() > 5 and len(set(y_new[m])) == 2: parts[name] = eer(y_new[m], score(head, X_new[m]))
    wts = {"asv": 0.25, "direct": 0.25, "replay": 0.5} if "replay" in parts else {"asv": 0.5, "direct": 0.5}
    return sum(wts[k] * parts[k] for k in wts) / sum(wts.values()), parts

head = SpoofHead().to(dev)
opt = torch.optim.AdamW(head.parameters(), lr=1e-3, weight_decay=1e-4)
bce = nn.BCEWithLogitsLoss()
EPOCHS, STEPS, BS, PATIENCE = 30, 150, 128, 8
best, bad, log = 9.0, 0, []
os.makedirs("/kaggle/working/spoof_v2", exist_ok=True)
for ep in range(EPOCHS):
    head.train(); idx = torch.multinomial(wt, STEPS * BS, replacement=True).to(dev); tot = 0
    for k in range(STEPS):
        b = idx[k * BS:(k + 1) * BS]
        opt.zero_grad(); loss = bce(head(Xg[b].float()), yg[b]); loss.backward(); opt.step(); tot += loss.item()
    comp, parts = dev_score(head)
    log.append({"epoch": ep + 1, "loss": tot / STEPS, "dev_composite_eer": comp, **{"dev_" + k: v for k, v in parts.items()}})
    print(f"epoch {ep+1:2d} loss {tot/STEPS:.4f} | dev composite EER {comp*100:5.2f}% | " + " ".join(f"{k} {v*100:.1f}%" for k, v in parts.items()))
    if comp < best:
        best, bad = comp, 0; torch.save(head.state_dict(), "/kaggle/working/spoof_v2/spoof_head_v2.pt")
    else:
        bad += 1
        if bad >= PATIENCE: print("early stop"); break
json.dump(log, open("/kaggle/working/spoof_v2/train_log_v2.json", "w"), indent=1)


# ===== Cell 5: honest evaluation (run for the old head and the new head) =====
def evaluate(head, name):
    res = {"name": name}
    sets = {}
    if Xev_a is not None: sets["ASVspoof eval (cached features)"] = (yev_a.astype(int), score(head, Xev_a))
    if Xit_a is not None: sets["In-the-Wild (cached features)"] = (yit_a.astype(int), score(head, Xit_a))
    for k, (y, s) in sets.items():
        lo, hi = boot_ci(y, s); res[k] = {"EER_%": round(eer(y, s) * 100, 2), "EER_CI": lo, "AUC": round(float(roc_auc_score(y, s)), 3), "n": int(len(y))}
    # threshold chosen on the new dev set (direct + replayed pooled)
    d = (meta.split == "dev").values & ((meta.condition == "direct").values | meta.condition.str.startswith("replayed").values)
    thr = eer_threshold(y_new[d], score(head, X_new[d]))
    res["threshold_from_new_dev"] = round(thr, 3)
    for cnd in ["direct"] + REPLAY_DIRS:
        mk = masks(meta, cnd); s_all = score(head, X_new)
        pairs = {"held-out speakers + seen engine (edge-tts)": ("real: held-out LibriSpeech speakers", "fake: edge-tts, unseen sentences"),
                 "unseen engine (gTTS)": (None, "fake: gTTS (unseen engine)")}
        for pname, (rk, fk) in pairs.items():
            real = mk["real: held-out LibriSpeech speakers"] | mk["real: your held-out lines"] if rk is None else mk[rk]
            fake = mk[fk]
            if real.sum() < 5 or fake.sum() < 5: continue
            y = np.concatenate([np.zeros(real.sum()), np.ones(fake.sum())]).astype(int); s = np.concatenate([s_all[real], s_all[fake]])
            lo, hi = boot_ci(y, s)
            res[f"{cnd} | {pname}"] = {"EER_%": round(eer(y, s) * 100, 1), "EER_CI": lo, "AUC": round(float(roc_auc_score(y, s)), 3), "AUC_CI": hi, "n_real": int(real.sum()), "n_fake": int(fake.sum())}
        res[f"{cnd} | share flagged as fake at dev threshold"] = {k: round(float((s_all[m] >= thr).mean()), 3) for k, m in mk.items() if m.sum()}
    return res

head2 = SpoofHead().to(dev); head2.load_state_dict(torch.load("/kaggle/working/spoof_v2/spoof_head_v2.pt")); head2.eval()
results = {"v2": evaluate(head2, "spoof head v2")}
old_paths = glob.glob("/kaggle/input/**/spoof_head.pt", recursive=True)
if old_paths:
    head1 = SpoofHead().to(dev); head1.load_state_dict(torch.load(old_paths[0])); head1.eval()
    results["v1"] = evaluate(head1, "spoof head v1 (Phase 1)")
for k, v in results.items():
    print("\n=====", v["name"], "=====")
    for kk, vv in v.items():
        if kk != "name": print(f"{kk:70s} {vv}")
json.dump(results, open("/kaggle/working/spoof_v2/results_v2.json", "w"), indent=1)
print("\nlayer weights (which wav2vec2 layers matter):", np.round(torch.softmax(head2.w, 0).detach().cpu().numpy(), 3))
```

**Design choices, in plain words:**
- **Every batch is half old ASVspoof data and half your new data**, with real and fake balanced inside each half. Without that, 25,000 ASVspoof examples would drown your few hundred new ones.
- **Replayed clips count 3 times as much** as direct ones (they are the case that matters), and the noise / phone-band / reverb copies count half. Those extra copies are made for **training clips only**.
- **Early stopping uses a composite** of three dev error rates (ASVspoof dev, new dev direct, new dev replayed), weighted toward replayed. The new dev set is tiny (about 54 clips per condition), so this signal is noisy.
- **Test data was never touched in training:** held-out LibriSpeech speakers, your held-out lines, unseen sentences from edge-tts, and gTTS (an engine the model never saw).
- **Cell 5 scores both the new and the old head** with identical code, so you get a fair before/after.

## Step 3: How to read the results (Step E)

The notebook prints, for v1 and v2: EER on ASVspoof eval (cached; v1 was 2.58%) and In-the-Wild (v1 was 33.3%), and for **direct** and **replayed** audio: EER and AUC with 95% intervals for "held-out speakers versus unseen sentences of edge-tts" and "versus the unseen gTTS engine", plus the **share flagged as fake** at a threshold chosen on the new dev set. That last line answers your original question: how often your own held-out recordings are still flagged.

| Result | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ASVspoof eval stays near 2.6% | The old skill was not lost |
| Replay AUC well above 0.5 (say above 0.8) for both seen and unseen engines | The speaker-to-microphone case is workable. Install v2 |
| Replay AUC stays around 0.5-0.65 | The speaker-to-microphone path destroys the cues. That is a real finding for the report. The fusion network will have to lean on the other streams |
| "Your held-out lines flagged as fake" is low (say under 20%) | Your live voice should stop looking fake |
| Direct AUC high but unseen-engine (gTTS) much lower than edge-tts | The model memorised one TTS engine rather than learning "fake speech" |

**Honest limits to write in the report:** the new test sets are small (60 real and 24 or 80 fake clips), so the intervals will be wide; there are only two TTS engines and one real speaker besides LibriSpeech; the model is trained once with one seed; and results are for this laptop's speaker and microphone.

## Step 4: Install v2 locally and rerun the checks

1. Download `spoof_head_v2.pt`, `results_v2.json` and `train_log_v2.json` from `/kaggle/working/spoof_v2/` (click **Save Version** first so they persist).
2. In `audio/models/`: rename the current `spoof_head.pt` to `spoof_head_v1.pt` (your backup), then save the new file as `spoof_head.pt`. That way you don't have to change any code.
3. Rerun `python -m audio.channel_check` and `python -m audio.replay_test` (stop the server first). In the old model, your voice scored 0.91-0.93 and replayed real versus fake gave AUC 0.325. Those are the two numbers to beat.
4. Then restart the server and look at the live page while speaking. Your own voice should now score low.

3. The two local checks (`channel_check` and `replay_test`) after installing v2.
4. The skipped-windows count and the latency breakdown from the live page, which I still need.

## Reading your spoof v2 results: promising, but too good to trust yet

### What the v2 notebook showed

| Check | v1 (Phase 1) | v2 (new) |
|---|---|---|
| **Replay**, held-out speakers vs edge-tts (seen engine) | EER 34.4%, AUC 0.76 | EER **0.0%**, AUC 1.0 |
| **Replay**, unseen gTTS engine | EER 30.0%, AUC 0.77 | EER **3.5%**, AUC 0.997 |
| **Direct**, unseen gTTS engine | EER 13.1% | EER 0.8% |
| Your held-out lines flagged as fake (replay) | 50% | 0% (but see below) |
| ASVspoof dev EER (training log) | 0.58% | 0.3-0.4% |
| In-the-Wild | EER 27% | EER 54% (**n = 30 only**, CI 15-75%) |

The checks you pasted last (`channel_check`, `replay_test`) show the **old** head again: same numbers as before (your voice 0.91 and 0.93, replay AUC 0.415, within noise of the earlier 0.325). So v2 isn't installed locally yet. That's expected, it just means those two checks tell us nothing about v2.

### Why I don't trust the near-perfect numbers yet

1. **The dev set is trivially easy:** direct and replay dev EER are **0.0% from epoch 1**, so early stopping had no real signal, and the loss ended around 0.001.
2. **The probabilities are saturated.** `threshold_from_new_dev` came out as **1.0**, which means the real/fake boundary sits where the model outputs exactly 1.0. That's why the "share flagged" line contradicts the EER: replayed gTTS has EER 3.5% yet only 2.5% of it was flagged at that threshold. The ranking is good, but the operating point is broken.
3. **Language and content are mixed up with the label.** Your real data is mostly English book reading (LibriSpeech), while a third of the fakes are Hindi and a third Hinglish. The only real Hindi and Hinglish speech is your own 54 clips (12 in the test split). The model can score well by learning "English book reader = real, scam-line text in Hindi = fake" without hearing anything about synthetic speech. The same goes for text: fakes read my scam and bank lines, and LibriSpeech reads literature.
4. **Two evaluation inputs are missing or wrong:** the output has **no ASVspoof eval line** at all, and In-the-Wild has only 30 clips (v1 earlier used 3,000). So I can't yet say whether v2 kept its general skill or traded it away. The 54% on 30 clips hints at a loss, but the interval is huge.

None of this means v2 is bad. It may be genuinely better on the replay channel. It means this evaluation can't distinguish "learned fake speech" from "learned our dataset's quirks".

## Step 1: Audit it (Cell 6, about 5 minutes)

Add this as a sixth cell in the same notebook session. It prints, per language and per class, the mean raw logit and the share flagged, plus a within-language AUC where both classes exist. It switches to **raw logits** so the saturation problem disappears, fits a calibration (Platt scaling) on pooled dev, and lists where the ASVspoof eval and In-the-Wild features really are. I tested the table logic on stand-in data and syntax-checked the rest; the model parts are untested.

```python
# ===== Cell 6: audit the v2 result (confounds, saturation, calibration) =====
from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression

@torch.no_grad()
def logits(head, X, bs=512):
    head.eval(); out = []
    for i in range(0, len(X), bs):
        out.append(head(torch.tensor(X[i:i + bs]).float().to(dev)).cpu().numpy())
    return np.concatenate(out)

def audit_table(L_all, meta, y_new, REPLAY_DIRS, name):
    print("\n=====", name, "=====")
    pool = (meta.split == "dev").values & ((meta.condition == "direct").values | meta.condition.str.startswith("replayed").values)
    thr = eer_threshold(y_new[pool], L_all[pool])
    print("threshold on raw logits (from pooled new dev):", round(thr, 2), "| logit range on all clips:", round(float(L_all.min()), 1), "to", round(float(L_all.max()), 1))
    for cnd in ["direct"] + REPLAY_DIRS:
        base = ((meta.condition == cnd).values) & meta.split.isin(["test", "test_unseen_engine"]).values
        print(f"--- {cnd}: test clips by language and class (mean logit | share flagged as fake) ---")
        for lang in ["english", "hinglish", "hindi"]:
            line = []
            for c in ["real", "fake"]:
                m = base & (meta.lang == lang).values & (meta.cls == c).values
                line.append(f"{c}: n={int(m.sum()):3d} " + (f"mean={L_all[m].mean():6.1f} flagged={np.mean(L_all[m] >= thr)*100:3.0f}%" if m.sum() else "-"))
            r = base & (meta.lang == lang).values & (meta.cls == "real").values
            f = base & (meta.lang == lang).values & (meta.cls == "fake").values
            auc = f"AUC={roc_auc_score(np.r_[np.zeros(r.sum()), np.ones(f.sum())], np.r_[L_all[r], L_all[f]]):.3f}" if r.sum() >= 5 and f.sum() >= 5 else "AUC=n/a (need >=5 of each class)"
            print(f"  {lang:9s} | " + " | ".join(line) + " | " + auc)
    # Platt scaling: turns logits into probabilities that are not saturated
    lr = LogisticRegression(C=1.0).fit(L_all[pool].reshape(-1, 1), y_new[pool])
    cal = {"a": float(lr.coef_[0][0]), "c": float(lr.intercept_[0]), "logit_threshold": thr}
    print("Platt calibration on pooled dev:", {k: round(v, 3) for k, v in cal.items()})
    return cal

cal2 = audit_table(logits(head2, X_new), meta, y_new, REPLAY_DIRS, "spoof head v2")
if old_paths:
    audit_table(logits(head1, X_new), meta, y_new, REPLAY_DIRS, "spoof head v1")
json.dump(cal2, open("/kaggle/working/spoof_v2/calibration_v2.json", "w"), indent=1)

# where are the full ASVspoof eval and In-the-Wild features? (Cell 5 printed In-the-Wild with only n=30 and no eval line)
for n in ["eval", "itw"]:
    ps = glob.glob(f"/kaggle/input/**/{n}_X.npy", recursive=True)
    print(n, [(p, np.load(p, mmap_mode="r").shape) for p in ps] or "NOT FOUND in the attached datasets")
```

**How to read it:**

| What you see | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **English-only AUC high** in replay (real LibriSpeech vs English fakes) | The model really separates real from fake speech, at least for English |
| English AUC high, but **Hindi/Hinglish "real: n = 12 only"** or AUC n/a | We can't tell for Hindi and Hinglish yet. We need more real recordings in those languages |
| English-only AUC **near 0.5** | The perfect score came from the language/content mix-up, and v2 is not trustworthy |
| Real logits around -10 and fake around +10, flagged shares sensible with the logit threshold | Saturation was just a thresholding artifact. Use the logit threshold and the Platt calibration |
| `eval` or `itw` shows **NOT FOUND** or the wrong shape | Those features aren't in the attached dataset. Add your Phase 1 features dataset, or tell me where they live |

If the last cell finds `eval_X.npy` (20,000 rows) and a full `itw_X.npy` (3,000 rows), rerun Cell 5 after setting `Xev_a, yev_a` and `Xit_a, yit_a` from those paths. That gives the ASVspoof eval and In-the-Wild numbers we need to know whether v2 lost general skill.

## What I'd expect, and the decision rule

- **If English-only replay AUC is high and your live voice scores low and an unfamiliar TTS voice scores high:** v2 is real progress. Keep it, report it with the caveats below, and add the calibration.
- **If the audit shows the result came from language or content:** we fix the data, not the model: record 20-30 more real Hindi and Hinglish lines (non-scam text, read naturally, then replayed), and generate English-only and non-scam-text fakes so real and fake match in language and content.
- **If the live voice is still high:** the problem is in something about live microphone audio itself, and I'd look at the exact capture path (browser sample-rate conversion, the audio worklet) next. I'd want that evidence before guessing.

## What to say in the report so far
Be plain about three things: (1) the speaker-to-microphone path destroyed the cues the Phase 1 detector used (replay AUC about 0.33-0.42 for v1); (2) the retraining data is small (two TTS engines, one real speaker plus LibriSpeech); (3) v2 results are on held-out data from the same laptop and channel, with interval estimates, and are not a general guarantee. The "unseen generator" claim rests on one engine (gTTS).

## Result: your original problem is fixed on the data we can trust, with two caveats and a few remaining checks

### What the numbers say now

| Check | Before (v1) | Now (v2) | Can we trust it? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ASVspoof eval** (20,000 clips) | EER 2.44% (CI 2.1-2.7) | EER **3.04%** (CI 2.7-3.5) | Yes. A small cost, so the old skill is mostly kept |
| **Replay**, unseen engine (gTTS), held-out speakers | EER 30.0%, AUC 0.77 | EER 3.5%, AUC **0.997** | Mostly (see caveats) |
| **Replay, English only** (real LibriSpeech vs English fakes) | AUC 0.716 | AUC **0.996** | Mostly |
| Your Hindi test clips flagged as fake, direct | 92% | 0% | Partly (your voice was in training) |
| Live-voice check (`channel_check`) | 0.91 near, 0.93 far | **0.01 near, 0.13 far** | Contaminated (below) |
| `replay_test` AUC | 0.33-0.42 | 1.000 direct, 0.975 replay | **Contaminated** (below) |

The In-the-Wild numbers are unusable: the dataset you attached holds only **30** clips (`itw_X.npy` has shape (30, 13, 768)), so the interval spans 15-75%. The ASVspoof eval features are stored as `eval_random_20000_X.npy`, which is why Cell 5 initially missed them; the numbers above are the correct 20,000.

### Two of your checks are contaminated, so don't quote them
- **`channel_check` on your own voice:** those 54 clips are the same recordings the model was trained on (my split puts most of your lines in training). "Your voice scores low" may simply mean the model learned your voice is real. That's a speaker shortcut, not a general fix.
- **`replay_test`:** it samples real clips from all 40 LibriSpeech speakers (28 were in training) and fakes from all edge-tts clips (70% were in training). The 0.975 is not independent.

The legitimate evidence is the held-out sets in Cell 5 and Cell 6: unseen LibriSpeech speakers and an unseen TTS engine, through the replay channel, with intervals.

### The language worry is smaller than I thought
My concern was that the model might learn "Hindi means fake". Cell 6 argues against it: your real Hindi test clips have a mean logit of -19 (0% flagged), and the real Hindi was never in training. (That's a quirk of my split: it put all your Hindi lines in dev and test.) But **English-only** separation is also very high (AUC 0.995 direct, 0.996 replay), and that comparison has real speakers on one side and TTS on the other in the same language.

What remains open: content (books vs. my scam lines), whether your voice shortcut matters, and Hinglish (no real Hinglish in the test split, so no number).

### The threshold problem, in plain terms
The model's outputs are on a huge scale (logits from -45 to +40), so "probability" saturates at 0 or 1. The threshold picked from dev (9.56) is too high: on replay, unseen-engine fakes have a mean logit of only about 0 to 4, so only 21-25% of them get flagged, while real speech sits near -24. A sensible threshold is somewhere between those, and the right way to pick it is from real speech: set it just above the highest realistic real scores (say the 98th percentile of real replayed logits), then check how many fakes that catches.

## Step 1: A content-matched, unseen-everything test (about 30 minutes, all local)

This removes the content confound by having **the same sentences** spoken by real people and by TTS: real clips come from LibriSpeech speakers never used in training, fakes are TTS of the same transcripts from gTTS and from edge-tts voices not used in training. Everything is trimmed, level-matched and MP3-equalised like the training data, then scored directly and after a speaker-to-microphone replay. It prints raw-logit statistics, AUCs and a candidate threshold.

First replace `audio/spoof_stream.py` with this version. It adds a raw `score_logit()` and makes `score_chunk()` return a calibrated probability, so the backend needs no change. (It assumes your Phase 1 file had the same structure as the one I gave you; if you added extra lines, re-add them.) Put `calibration_v2.json` from Kaggle into `audio/models/` next to `spoof_head.pt`.

```python
import json, os
from pathlib import Path
import numpy as np, torch, torch.nn as nn
from transformers import Wav2Vec2Model, Wav2Vec2FeatureExtractor

SR, LEN = 16000, 64000
MODELS = Path(__file__).parent / "models"
dev = os.environ.get("RV_SPOOF_DEVICE") or ("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")
print("Using device:", dev)

fe = Wav2Vec2FeatureExtractor.from_pretrained("facebook/wav2vec2-base")
w2v = Wav2Vec2Model.from_pretrained("facebook/wav2vec2-base").to(dev).eval()


class SpoofHead(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, L=13, D=768):
        super().__init__()
        self.w = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(L))
        self.net = nn.Sequential(nn.LayerNorm(D), nn.Linear(D, 256), nn.GELU(), nn.Dropout(0.3), nn.Linear(256, 1))

    def forward(self, x):
        a = torch.softmax(self.w, 0)[None, :, None]
        return self.net((x * a).sum(1)).squeeze(-1)


head = SpoofHead().to(dev)
head.load_state_dict(torch.load(MODELS / "spoof_head.pt", map_location=dev))
head.eval()

_cal_path = MODELS / "calibration_v2.json"
CAL = json.load(open(_cal_path)) if _cal_path.exists() else {"a": 0.5}
A = float(CAL.get("a", 0.5))              # slope of the calibration (Platt scaling on the logit)
SHIFT = float(CAL.get("shift", 0.0))      # the logit that should count as "50 % synthetic"; set it after choosing the threshold


@torch.no_grad()
def score_logit(wav):
    """Raw head output. Large negative = human-like, large positive = synthetic-like."""
    wav = np.asarray(wav, dtype=np.float32)
    if len(wav) < LEN:
        wav = np.pad(wav, (0, LEN - len(wav)))
    x = fe([wav[:LEN]], sampling_rate=SR, return_tensors="pt").input_values.to(dev)
    hs = w2v(x, output_hidden_states=True).hidden_states
    f = torch.stack([h.mean(1) for h in hs], 1).float()
    return float(head(f).item())


def score_chunk(wav):
    """Calibrated probability (0-1) that the audio is synthetic. Same name and range as before, so the backend is unchanged."""
    z = score_logit(wav)
    return float(1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-A * (z - SHIFT))))
```

Then save this as `audio/paired_test.py` and run `python -m audio.paired_test` from `rakshavoice/`. It needs internet (TTS), ffmpeg, your speakers and microphone, and the **server stopped**. I syntax-checked it and tested the transcript-parsing helper on stand-in data, but the TTS, replay and scoring parts have not been run. Set the speaker volume to about 60% and keep the room quiet. It plays about 120 clips (roughly 11 minutes).

```python
import asyncio, glob, os, random, re, shutil, subprocess, time
import numpy as np, pandas as pd, soundfile as sf, librosa, sounddevice as sd
from sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score
from audio.spoof_stream import score_logit

SR, SEC, PAD = 16000, 4, 0.5
ROOT = "voice/data/LibriSpeech/test-clean"; OUT = "audio/data_v2/paired"
random.seed(1); rng = np.random.RandomState(1)
os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
assert shutil.which("ffmpeg"), "ffmpeg is needed (winget install Gyan.FFmpeg)"

def pick_sentences(n_per_speaker=5):
    out = []
    spk = sorted(os.listdir(ROOT))[32:]                                  # the 8 held-out speakers (same split as prep_v2)
    for s in spk:
        items = []
        for t in glob.glob(f"{ROOT}/{s}/*/*.trans.txt"):
            for line in open(t, encoding="utf-8"):
                uid, text = line.strip().split(" ", 1)
                if 8 <= len(text.split()) <= 25:
                    items.append((uid, text.capitalize(), os.path.join(os.path.dirname(t), uid + ".flac")))
        random.shuffle(items); out += [(s,) + it for it in items[:n_per_speaker]]
    return out

def voiced_crop(y):
    y, _ = librosa.effects.trim(y, top_db=30); n = SEC * SR
    if len(y) < int(0.75 * n): return None
    if len(y) < n: y = np.pad(y, (0, n - len(y)))
    s = rng.randint(0, len(y) - n + 1); y = y[s:s + n]
    return (y / (np.max(np.abs(y)) + 1e-9) * 0.5).astype(np.float32)

def mp3_roundtrip(y, name):
    p = f"{OUT}/{name}.wav"; sf.write(p, y, SR)
    subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-y", "-loglevel", "error", "-i", p, "-ar", "24000", "-b:a", "48k", p + ".mp3"], check=True)
    subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-y", "-loglevel", "error", "-i", p + ".mp3", "-ac", "1", "-ar", str(SR), p], check=True)
    os.remove(p + ".mp3"); return sf.read(p, dtype="float32")[0]

def tts_to_wav(mp3_path):
    subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-y", "-loglevel", "error", "-i", mp3_path, "-ac", "1", "-ar", str(SR), mp3_path + ".wav"], check=True)
    y = sf.read(mp3_path + ".wav", dtype="float32")[0]; os.remove(mp3_path + ".wav"); os.remove(mp3_path); return y

async def edge_clip(text, voice, path):
    import edge_tts
    await edge_tts.Communicate(text, voice).save(path)

def replay(y):
    x = np.concatenate([np.zeros(int(PAD * SR)), y, np.zeros(int(PAD * SR))]).astype(np.float32).reshape(-1, 1)
    rec = sd.playrec(x, samplerate=SR, channels=1, dtype="float32"); sd.wait()
    return rec[int(PAD * SR): int(PAD * SR) + len(y), 0]

rows = []
sents = pick_sentences(); print(len(sents), "sentences from held-out speakers")
for k, (spk, uid, text, flac) in enumerate(sents):
    y = sf.read(flac, dtype="float32")[0]
    real = voiced_crop(y)
    if real is None: continue
    rows.append(("real", "librispeech", f"real_{uid}", mp3_roundtrip(real, f"real_{uid}")))
    from gtts import gTTS
    try:
        gTTS(text=text, lang="en", tld="co.in").save(f"{OUT}/g.mp3"); f = voiced_crop(tts_to_wav(f"{OUT}/g.mp3"))
        if f is not None: rows.append(("fake", "gtts", f"gtts_{uid}", mp3_roundtrip(f, f"gtts_{uid}")))
    except Exception as e: print("gTTS skip", uid, type(e).__name__)
    voice = ["en-US-AriaNeural", "en-GB-SoniaNeural"][k % 2]
    try:
        asyncio.run(edge_clip(text, voice, f"{OUT}/e.mp3")); f = voiced_crop(tts_to_wav(f"{OUT}/e.mp3"))
        if f is not None: rows.append(("fake", "edge_new_voice", f"edge_{uid}", mp3_roundtrip(f, f"edge_{uid}")))
    except Exception as e: print("edge skip", uid, type(e).__name__)
    time.sleep(0.5)
print(pd.Series([r[1] for r in rows]).value_counts().to_dict(), "clips; replaying them now (about", int(len(rows) * 5.6 / 60), "minutes)")

res = []
for cls, grp, name, y in rows:
    res.append({"cls": cls, "group": grp, "name": name, "direct": score_logit(y), "replay": score_logit(replay(y))})
df = pd.DataFrame(res); df.to_csv("audio/data_v2/paired_scores.csv", index=False)

for cond in ("direct", "replay"):
    real = df[df.cls == "real"][cond].values
    print(f"\n=== {cond} (raw logits; negative = human-like) ===")
    print(f"real            n={len(real):3d} mean={real.mean():6.1f}  median={np.median(real):6.1f}  95th pct={np.percentile(real, 95):6.1f}  99th pct={np.percentile(real, 99):6.1f}")
    for g in ("gtts", "edge_new_voice"):
        f = df[df.group == g][cond].values
        if len(f) < 3: continue
        auc = roc_auc_score(np.r_[np.zeros(len(real)), np.ones(len(f))], np.r_[real, f])
        print(f"fake {g:14s} n={len(f):3d} mean={f.mean():6.1f}  median={np.median(f):6.1f}  5th pct={np.percentile(f, 5):6.1f}  AUC vs real={auc:.3f}")
rr = df[df.cls == "real"]["replay"].values; thr = float(np.percentile(rr, 98))
print(f"\nCandidate threshold = 98th percentile of REAL replayed logits = {thr:.1f}")
for g in ("gtts", "edge_new_voice"):
    f = df[df.group == g]["replay"].values
    if len(f): print(f"  at that threshold: {g} detected {np.mean(f >= thr) * 100:3.0f}% | real flagged {np.mean(rr >= thr) * 100:3.0f}%")
```

**How to read it:**

| Result | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Replay AUC above about 0.9 for both gTTS and the new edge voices | The fix holds on same-text, unseen-speaker, unseen-engine audio. Strong evidence |
| AUC high for gTTS but low for the new edge voices (or the reverse) | The model learned engine-specific habits. More TTS variety needed |
| AUC near 0.5-0.7 | The earlier perfect scores came from content or dataset quirks. We fix the data |
| "Candidate threshold" with a good detection rate (say above 70%) at about 2% real false alarms | Use that number as the operating point |

## Step 2: Test live with people and voices that were not in training

This is the test that answers your original question, and nothing in training touched it:
1. **A different real speaker:** ask a family member or friend (with their consent) to speak into your laptop microphone through the live page for 10 seconds. Note the spoof score.
2. **A different TTS voice, replayed:** play a text-to-speech clip from your phone (any TTS app or website, a voice we didn't use) near the microphone. Note the score.
3. **Yourself again, with new sentences:** read three new lines in English, Hinglish and Hindi that are *not* scam lines (news-style text), so it isn't the same content as training.

Expect: the other person's live voice low, your new lines low, the TTS clip high. If the other person scores high, the model learned "your voice = real" and we retrain with more speakers.

## Step 3 (optional): In-the-Wild properly
Only 30 clips are in your features dataset, so v1 and v2 can't be compared on it. If you want the comparison (a good external check for the report), attach the In-the-Wild dataset on Kaggle and tell me its folder layout (`!find /kaggle/input -maxdepth 3 -iname "*meta*"`), and I'll write the 3,000-clip extraction. I'd do this after Steps 1 and 2.

## Then: set the operating point
After Step 1 prints a candidate threshold, add `"shift": <that number>` to `audio/models/calibration_v2.json`. Then `score_chunk()` returns 0.5 exactly at that threshold, and the page's gauge will be meaningful. Also change the label under the spoof gauge from "uncalibrated" to "calibrated on this laptop's microphone path".

## What to say in the report
- Phase 1 head failed on speaker-to-microphone audio (replay AUC 0.33-0.42, own voice 91% flagged); v2 retrained on in-channel data gets replay AUC 0.997 on held-out speakers and an unseen TTS engine, with ASVspoof eval EER 3.0% (was 2.4%).
- Limitations: two TTS engines for training, one real speaker besides LibriSpeech, results on one laptop's speaker and microphone path, and In-the-Wild not re-evaluated (30 clips only).

## What your two new tests say

### `paired_test.py` (same sentences for real and fake, unseen speakers, unseen engine)

| Replay (speaker to microphone) | Real speech | gTTS (engine never trained on) | edge-tts voices never trained on |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mean raw logit | -26.3 | -11.2 | -21.6 |
| AUC against real | | **0.976** | **0.699** |
| Detected at the candidate threshold (-14.8), with 3% real false alarms | | **80%** | **15%** |

Direct (clean files): gTTS AUC 0.965, new edge voices AUC 0.858.

**Reading:** this is the first test free of the content, speaker and engine shortcuts, and it holds for **gTTS**. That's a real improvement over v1 (replay AUC about 0.77). But it **doesn't generalise to other high-quality neural voices** (the two unseen Edge voices). Training had only four Edge voices (two Indian-English, two Hindi), so the model partly learned those voices. Modern voice clones are closer to those neural voices than to gTTS, so assume real-world detection of good clones will be weak until the training voices are more varied.

### Your live test: "0-5% for all three"
Your own new lines and another person's voice scoring 0-5% is the correct answer. The TTS voice from your phone also scoring 0-5% is the weakness above showing up live: it wasn't detected. (At the moment the gauge's 50% point is at logit 0, and replayed fakes sit around -11 to -22, so they read near 0%.)

### A thing to understand before choosing a threshold
The threshold depends on **how the audio reached the microphone**:

| Real speech | 95th percentile | 99th percentile |
|---|---|---|
| Direct (clean file) | -3.8 | +8.4 |
| Replayed through a speaker | -16.3 | -14.7 |

A threshold near -15 suits the **speakerphone** path. But applied to a person speaking directly into the laptop microphone, it would flag far more than 5% of real speech, and the 0-5% readings you just saw would turn into false alarms. Your real product scenario is a call on speakerphone, so that path is the one to calibrate. Direct-microphone speech needs a different (higher) threshold. A small "speakerphone / direct microphone" switch in the page can handle this honestly.

## "I don't know how to find the latency and skipped windows"

They are on the page itself, in the top row of three panels, while it is listening (after the new backend sends its first result):
- **INFERENCE LATENCY** panel: the big number is the total in milliseconds. The small grey line under it is the per-stage breakdown, like "Spoof 190 · speech 700 · intent 25 · voice 40 ms".
- **CONNECTION** panel: the line "Local FastAPI WebSocket · skipped windows: N". N is the skipped-window count.

Speak for 30 seconds, then read those two things without clicking Stop. You told me the total was 1,837 ms; I still need the breakdown line and N.

```python
        jobs = [loop.run_in_executor(None, _timed, score_logit, window),
```

```python
        (logit, t_spoof), (text, intent, t_asr, t_int) = res[0], res[1]
        p_fake = 1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-SPOOF_A * (float(logit) - SPOOF_SHIFT)))
```

## Step 3: Spoof v3, more voice variety (timeboxed to one day)

I recommend doing this **once**, because the demo's "AI clone" scenario depends on it and the current detector fails on good neural voices. The idea: train on **many** Edge English voices (reserving the two test voices), with sentences that are LibriSpeech transcripts of the training speakers, so real and fake speech match in content and the model can't learn the voices.

**3a. Generate (needs internet, about 20 minutes):** save as `audio/make_tts_v2.py`, run `python -m audio.make_tts_v2`. It lists the English Edge voices that exist, picks 10 at random (excluding `en-US-AriaNeural` and `en-GB-SoniaNeural`), and makes 20 clips per voice; the last two voices form the dev set. I syntax-checked it; the TTS call itself is untested. It needs ffmpeg.

```python
import asyncio, glob, os, random, subprocess, time
import numpy as np, pandas as pd, soundfile as sf, librosa

ROOT = "voice/data/LibriSpeech/test-clean"; OUT = "audio/data_v2/fake/edge_multi"
HELD_OUT = {"en-US-AriaNeural", "en-GB-SoniaNeural"}
N_VOICES, N_PER_VOICE, SR = 10, 20, 16000
random.seed(2); os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)

def sentences(speakers, n):
    items = []
    for s in speakers:
        for t in glob.glob(f"{ROOT}/{s}/*/*.trans.txt"):
            for line in open(t, encoding="utf-8"):
                uid, text = line.strip().split(" ", 1)
                if 8 <= len(text.split()) <= 25: items.append(text.capitalize())
    random.shuffle(items); return items[:n]

async def run():
    import edge_tts
    allv = await edge_tts.list_voices()
    en = sorted({v["ShortName"] for v in allv if v["Locale"].startswith("en-") and v["ShortName"] not in HELD_OUT and "Multilingual" not in v["ShortName"]})
    random.shuffle(en); voices = en[:N_VOICES]
    print("available English voices:", len(en), "| using:", voices)
    spk = sorted(os.listdir(ROOT)); train_s, dev_s = spk[:28], spk[28:32]
    manifest = []
    for vi, voice in enumerate(voices):
        split = "train" if vi < N_VOICES - 2 else "dev"                      # the last two voices form the dev set (unseen voices)
        for k, text in enumerate(sentences(train_s if split == "train" else dev_s, N_PER_VOICE)):
            name = f"{voice}_{k:03d}"; mp3 = f"{OUT}/{name}.mp3"; wav = f"{OUT}/{name}.wav"
            try:
                await edge_tts.Communicate(text, voice).save(mp3)
                subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-y", "-loglevel", "error", "-i", mp3, "-ac", "1", "-ar", str(SR), wav], check=True); os.remove(mp3)
                manifest.append({"path": wav, "voice": voice, "split": split, "text": text})
            except Exception as e:
                print("skip", name, type(e).__name__, e)
            await asyncio.sleep(0.2)
    pd.DataFrame(manifest).to_csv("audio/data_v2/manifest_fake_multi.csv", index=False)
    print(len(manifest), "fake clips written")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    asyncio.run(run())
```

**3b. Add them to the manifest (10 minutes):** save as `audio/prep_v2b.py`, run `python -m audio.prep_v2b`. It trims, level-matches and MP3-equalises the new fakes, adds 4 more real LibriSpeech clips for every training and dev speaker, and appends everything to `manifest_direct.csv`. I tested the appending logic on stand-in audio.

```python
import glob, os, random, shutil, subprocess
import numpy as np, pandas as pd, soundfile as sf, librosa

SR, SEC = 16000, 4
OUT = "audio/data_v2/direct"; os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
random.seed(3); rng = np.random.RandomState(3)
USE_MP3 = shutil.which("ffmpeg") is not None
man = pd.read_csv("audio/data_v2/manifest_direct.csv"); have = set(man.id)

def voiced_crop(y, sec=SEC):
    y, _ = librosa.effects.trim(y, top_db=30); n = int(sec * SR)
    if len(y) < int(0.75 * n): return None
    if len(y) < n: y = np.pad(y, (0, n - len(y)))
    s = rng.randint(0, len(y) - n + 1); y = y[s:s + n]
    return (y / (np.max(np.abs(y)) + 1e-9) * 0.5).astype(np.float32)

def read16k(path):
    y, sr = sf.read(path, dtype="float32", always_2d=True); y = y.mean(axis=1)
    return y if sr == SR else librosa.resample(y, orig_sr=sr, target_sr=SR)

def mp3_roundtrip(path):
    tmp = path + ".mp3"
    subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-y", "-loglevel", "error", "-i", path, "-ar", "24000", "-b:a", "48k", tmp], check=True)
    subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-y", "-loglevel", "error", "-i", tmp, "-ac", "1", "-ar", str(SR), path], check=True); os.remove(tmp)

rows = []
def add(cid, y, cls, source, split, lang, who):
    if cid in have: return
    p = f"{OUT}/{cid}.wav"; sf.write(p, y, SR)
    if USE_MP3: mp3_roundtrip(p)
    rows.append({"id": cid, "path": p, "cls": cls, "source": source, "split": split, "lang": lang, "who": who, "condition": "direct"})

fm = pd.read_csv("audio/data_v2/manifest_fake_multi.csv")
for r in fm.itertuples():
    y = voiced_crop(read16k(r.path))
    if y is not None: add(f"fake_edgemulti_{os.path.basename(r.path)[:-4]}", y, "fake", "edge-multi", r.split, "english", r.voice)

ROOT = "voice/data/LibriSpeech/test-clean"; spk = sorted(os.listdir(ROOT))
for i, s in enumerate(spk[:32]):
    split = "train" if i < 28 else "dev"
    files = glob.glob(f"{ROOT}/{s}/*/*.flac"); random.shuffle(files); k = 0
    for f in files:
        y = voiced_crop(read16k(f))
        if y is not None: add(f"libri2_{s}_{k:02d}", y, "real", "librispeech", split, "english", s); k += 1
        if k >= 4: break

new = pd.DataFrame(rows); out = pd.concat([man, new], ignore_index=True)
out.to_csv("audio/data_v2/manifest_direct.csv", index=False)
print("MP3 equalisation:", "ON" if USE_MP3 else "OFF", "| added", len(new), "clips")
print(out.groupby(["cls", "source", "split"]).size().to_string())
```

**3c. Replay the new clips (about 30 minutes):** `python -m audio.replay_batch --limit 150` again, 2-3 times. It skips the ones already recorded, so it only does the roughly 310 new clips.

**3d. Then install the new head and rerun `python -m audio.paired_test`.

**Decision rule, decided now:** if the edge-new-voice replay AUC rises to about 0.85 or more with gTTS staying high, keep v3. If it stays below about 0.8, **stop**, report it as a limitation, and move on to the fusion network, because one more iteration won't change the conclusion and you have about seven weeks left.

## What to say in the report so far
The spoof detector reaches ASVspoof EER 3.0% and, on a content-matched replayed test with unseen speakers, AUC 0.976 for an unseen low-quality TTS engine but 0.70 for unseen high-quality neural voices. So detection of modern clones is unreliable, and the fusion design therefore must not depend on this stream alone.

## Results

| Replay (speaker to microphone) | v2 | v3 (installed now) |
|---|---|---|
| gTTS (engine never trained on), AUC | **0.976** | 0.666 |
| New Edge voices (not trained on), AUC | 0.699 | **0.942** |
| Detected at ~3% real false alarms | gTTS 80%, new Edge voices 15% | gTTS 6%, new Edge voices 48% |

By my rule (the new-voice AUC up, the gTTS AUC staying high), v3 half-passes, since gTTS fell. The pattern is the real finding: **the detector learns the TTS engines it was trained on, and what it generalises to changes from run to run.** v2 caught gTTS but not new Edge voices, and v3 is the reverse. That is a limitation to report with both tables.

## Decision: keep v3, stop retraining, move on

- Modern voice clones sound like neural voices, not like gTTS, so v3's strength (AUC 0.94, even in replay) matters more for your clone scenario.
- v3 is also more consistent across channels: real speech has a 95th percentile of 2.1 when played directly and 0.1 when replayed. So **one threshold works for both**, and you don't need the direct/speakerphone switch.
- Keep v2 as a backup named `spoof_head_v2a.pt`, and report v1, v2 and v3 in one table.
- Latency is fine: spoof 92 ms, speech 1,200 ms, intent 15 ms, and **skipped windows 0**. Speech-to-text dominates.

## Set the operating point (2 minutes)

Open `audio/models/calibration_v2.json` (the one from the v3 run) and add a shift of 0.9, which is the 98th percentile of real replayed speech. For example:
```json
{"a": 0.5, "c": 0.0, "logit_threshold": 1.0, "shift": 0.9}
```
Keep whatever `a` value is already in your file and only add `"shift": 0.9`. Now a score of 50% on the gauge means "at the threshold". Restart the server.

## Phase 5, step 1: log numbers only (30 minutes)

The fusion network needs sequences of scores from real sessions. The logger saves one line per window with the scores and your label (**no audio and no text**). I tested the backend part with stand-in models: the log lines contained only numbers. The page part was syntax-checked only.

**`backend/main.py`** (five edits, starting from your current logit version):

1. Directly above `app = FastAPI()` add:
```python
LOG_DIR = "logs/sessions"                  # numbers only: no audio and no text are ever written here
os.makedirs(LOG_DIR, exist_ok=True)
VALID_LABELS = {"normal", "scam", "clone", "unknown"}
```
2. Directly above `async def _process` add:
```python
def _log(state, rec):
    rec = {"session": state["session"], "label": state["label"], "step": state["step"], "t": round(time.time(), 2), **rec}
    state["step"] += 1
    with open(f"{LOG_DIR}/{state['session']}.jsonl", "a") as f:
        f.write(json.dumps(rec) + "\n")
```
3. In `_process`, in the silence branch, add a line before `await ws.send_json(... "silent": True ...)`:
```python
            _log(state, {"silent": True})
```
4. In `_process`, right after the line `lat["total"] = ...` add:
```python
        lab = intent.get("labels") or {}
        _log(state, {"silent": False, "spoof_logit": streams["spoof"]["logit"], "spoof_p": streams["spoof"]["score"],
                     "urgency": lab.get("urgency"), "authority": lab.get("authority"), "otp_pii": lab.get("otp_pii"), "payment": lab.get("payment"),
                     "voice_active": "voice" in streams, "voice_similarity": streams.get("voice", {}).get("similarity"), "latency_ms": lat["total"]})
```
5. In `ws_endpoint`, replace the `state = {...}` line with:
```python
    state = {"busy": False, "contact": None, "dropped": 0, "label": "unknown", "step": 0, "session": time.strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")}
```
and just above `if ctrl.get("type") == "select_contact":` add:
```python
                if ctrl.get("type") == "set_label":
                    lab = ctrl.get("label", "unknown")
                    state["label"] = lab if lab in VALID_LABELS else "unknown"
                    await ws.send_json({"type": "label_set", "label": state["label"]})
```

**`App.jsx`** (five edits):

1. Next to the other `useState` lines: `const [label, setLabel] = useState("unknown");`
2. Next to `contactRef`: `const labelRef = useRef("unknown");`
3. Above `function onContactChange` add:
```jsx
  function onLabelChange(event) {
    const value = event.target.value;
    setLabel(value);
    labelRef.current = value;
    const ws = wsRef.current;
    if (ws && ws.readyState === WebSocket.OPEN) {
      ws.send(JSON.stringify({ type: "set_label", label: value }));
    }
  }
```
4. In `ws.onopen`, after `sendContact(contactRef.current);` add `ws.send(JSON.stringify({ type: "set_label", label: labelRef.current }));`, and change `if (result.type === "contact_set") return;` to `if (result.type === "contact_set" || result.type === "label_set") return;`
5. Inside `<div className="buttons">`, before the Start/Stop button, add:
```jsx
          <label className="select-label" htmlFor="label">
            Session label (saved with the scores for training)
          </label>
          <select id="label" className="select" value={label} onChange={onLabelChange}>
            <option value="unknown">unknown</option>
            <option value="normal">normal call</option>
            <option value="scam">scam call</option>

```
Also add `logs/` to `.gitignore`.

[Thinking (summary)]
Explaining the benefits of using multiple voice actors.
[End of Thinking (summary)]

## Yes, and it will make the project stronger

Different people matter for three reasons:
- **The fusion network shouldn't learn "this is Vighnesh's voice."** Your Phase 1 detector already fell into that kind of shortcut once.
- **Your report can then claim results across several speakers.** Right now it's you plus LibriSpeech.
- **The voice check needs a real "different person" test**, and a family voice is the hardest case.

## How to do it

**Who:** 3-5 adults (family or friends), different ages and genders if possible, and ideally some who speak Hindi or Hinglish. Adults only.

**Consent first.** Read them this before they speak: *"This app listens through the microphone, analyses the audio live, and discards it. It saves only numbers, never your voice or the words. You can ask me to delete your session files, and you can stop at any time."* Get a yes (a message is fine). If you also want to enrol someone for the voice check, that needs the separate enrolment consent (`python -m voice.enroll_cli` shows it).

**What each person does** (about 15 minutes each):
- 3-4 **normal** sessions: talk naturally, or read ordinary call lines.
- 3-4 **scam** sessions: read your scam lines in their own voice, in different languages.
- Some of both played **directly** into the laptop, and some from a **phone speaker**.
- Press Start monitoring only after choosing the session label.

**Keep a tiny notes file**, `logs/sessions_notes.csv`, with one row per session: file name, person (P1, P2, ...), direct or phone. It's needed later to split the data **by person**, so the same person never appears in both training and testing.

## Cloned voices (the "clone" label)
Using different people for the *fake* class means different TTS voices reading scam scripts: use several voices that were **not** in the spoof training (any phone TTS app or website). Aim for 3-5 different voices, and note which ones in the same file.

## Optional: a stronger voice-check test
If one or two people agree to be enrolled: enrol them, then pick their name in the page and check that **you** speaking gives "does not match" and **they** speaking gives "matches". Report the similarity numbers. That's a real different-person test.

## What to send me
1. How many people and sessions you recorded.
2. The file list in `logs/sessions/` and the first 3 lines of one file.

## Doing it properly with your mother and brother

Both are adults, which is what you need. Family voices are also the **hardest honest test** for the voice check, because relatives can sound alike.

### 1. Before anyone speaks (5 minutes each)
Read this aloud and get a clear yes from each person: *"The app listens through the microphone, analyses the audio live and discards it. It saves only numbers, never your voice or your words. You can stop any time and ask me to delete your files."* If you also enrol them for the voice check, that needs the separate enrolment consent that `enroll_cli` shows. Tell them the scam lines are **role-play scripts**, so nothing real is being said.

### 2. Setup (same every time)
Quiet room, Chrome only, Edge, Brave and WhatsApp closed, laptop in the same spot, phone speaker volume about 60%. Start the server, open the page, and check that the page responds before they arrive.

### 3. Enrol them (optional but recommended, 10 minutes each)
Stop the server, or run `$env:RV_VOICE_DEVICE="cpu"` in the enrolment window, then `python -m voice.enroll_cli`. Use the names `mom` and `brother`. They read the consent statement themselves, you type YES, and they speak naturally for 8 seconds, three times (Hindi is fine). Restart the server afterwards.

### 4. Sessions per person (about 20 minutes)
Choose the **session label before pressing Start monitoring**, and keep each session to about 60 seconds.

| # | What | Label | Path |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1-2 | Talk naturally, or read the 6 normal lines from your recording list (2 per language, they choose their language) | normal | direct into the laptop |
| 3-4 | Read 6 scam lines (OTP, police, payment) | scam | direct |
| 5 | **A real phone call**: they call your phone, put it on speaker next to the laptop, and read normal lines | normal | phone call |
| 6 | Same, reading scam lines | scam | phone call |

Sessions 5 and 6 matter most: they capture the real telephone path that your demo uses.

### 5. Keep a notes file
Create `logs/sessions_notes.csv` and add a row after every session, so the data can later be split by person:
```
file,person,label,path
20261025_101530.jsonl,mom,normal,direct
20261025_101904.jsonl,mom,scam,phone_call
```
The file name is the newest file in `logs/sessions/`. Use `me`, `mom`, `brother` as person names.

### 6. Voice-check test (10 minutes)
In the page, pick `mom` in "The caller says they are" and speak for 10 seconds each as: mom (expect "matches"), brother (the hard one), you. Do the same with `brother` selected. Write down the **similarity** shown for each case, plus the same on a phone call. The threshold is 0.298; if brother and mom score above it against each other, report that honestly, because it's a real limitation for family-impersonation scams.

### 7. Privacy afterwards
Logs hold numbers only, and enrolled voiceprints stay on your laptop. Tell them how to ask for deletion: you delete their notes rows, their session files and their enrolment (`python -m voice.enroll_cli --delete mom`).

One session per voice. Write the voice name in the notes file, e.g. `person = tts_voiceA`.

## Read them one by one, as one continuous session

- **One session = one press of Start monitoring.** Read that session's 4 lines one after another, in the order shown in the table (for example N1, then N5, then N9, then N3), with a pause of about 2 seconds between lines. Don't press Stop between lines.
- **Say only the sentences.** Don't say the codes like "N1" or "S5" aloud.
- **Each session takes about 40 seconds** (about 8-10 seconds per line, plus the pauses). When the 4 lines are done, wait 3 seconds, then press **Stop monitoring**.
- **Then add the notes row** for that file, and start the next session.
- **Don't read all sessions in one go.** Every session needs a new Start monitoring, with its own label chosen first.
- **Phone-call sessions are the same:** one call per session, the 4 lines read in order during that call. Hang up after the last line, and press Stop.
- **If someone stumbles,** let it continue and don't restart. A little messiness is realistic.
